which puts us in a more complicated setup than before, because the sum cannot be simplified further until a specific code and noise model is given.
Nevertheless, this can be computed when a specific i.i.d. single-qubit Pauli noise model is given.
TheZZcoordinate returned by task-specific marginal ML decoding forXXmemory is thenzL∗∈arg​maxzL∈𝔽2kout⁡Pr⁡[zL∣s].z_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}\in\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{z_{\mathrm{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}}}\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right].(B.61)

## B.4Explicit Decoders for Gauss’s Law Codes

Now we come to the specific decoders used in our code capacity demonstrations inSectionIV.2.
For the depolarizing noise model (used in ourZZmemory andXXmemory experiments), we implement marginalized ML decoding, as discussed inSectionB.3.
Recall that the binary vector representation for the concatenated code is defined by the following information:
- •

The parity-check matrices,HinH_{\mathrm{in}}andHoutH_{\mathrm{out}};
- •

The coordinatization of inner logical equivalence classes byπinX\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{in}}andπinZ\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{in}};
- •

The coordinatization of outer logical equivalence classes byπoutX\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}andπoutZ\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}.

The parity-check matrixHoutH_{\mathrm{out}}is defined directly by the parity checks used by classical Gauss’s law codeCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}, as discussed inSectionIII.
ForHinH_{\mathrm{in}}, we use the standard parity checks of thedd-bit repetition code, namely,Hin≡(110⋯00011⋯00⋮⋮⋮⋱⋮⋮000⋯11).H_{\mathrm{in}}\equiv\begin{pmatrix}1&1&0&\cdots&0&0\\
0&1&1&\cdots&0&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&0&0&\cdots&1&1\end{pmatrix}.(B.62)

We also assume odddd, as inSectionIV.

For the inner logical equivalence classes, we useπinX​(xin(b))\displaystyle\pi_{\mathrm{in}}^{X}\left(x_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)≡{0,wt(xin(b))≡0(mod2),1,wt(xin(b))≡1(mod2),\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}0,&\operatorname*{wt}\left(x_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)\equiv 0\pmod{2},\\
1,&\operatorname*{wt}\left(x_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)\equiv 1\pmod{2},\end{cases}(B.63)πinZ​(zin(b))\displaystyle\pi_{\mathrm{in}}^{Z}\left(z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)≡{0,wt(zin(b))<12​d,1,wt(zin(b))>12​d,\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}0,&\operatorname*{wt}\left(z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)<\frac{1}{2}d,\\
1,&\operatorname*{wt}\left(z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)>\frac{1}{2}d,\end{cases}(B.64)

for everyxin(b),zin(b)∈𝔽2ninx_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)},z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{in}}}.
Another way to put this is as follows: define thebthb^{\mathrm{th}}block “inner” logical bit-flip and phase-flip operators byX¯(b)\displaystyle\overline{X}^{(b)}≅X⊗I⊗(nin−1),\displaystyle\cong X\otimes I^{\otimes(n_{\mathrm{in}}-1)},(B.65)Z¯(b)\displaystyle\overline{Z}^{(b)}≅Z⊗nin,\displaystyle\cong Z^{\otimes n_{\mathrm{in}}},(B.66)

where the RHS is assumed to act solely on thebthb^{\mathrm{th}}block.
Then the inner logicalXXcoordinate simply tells us whether the bit-flips from a Pauli error can be generated by the inner stabilizer generators (which would imply it isnota genuine error) or not—indeed, the two possible answers to this question are exactly separated byX¯(b)\overline{X}^{(b)}.
For the inner logicalZZcoordinate of the error, we simply look at whether a majority or minority of the qubits on blockbbare affected by a phase-flip, which is also exactly separated by the action ofZ¯(b)\overline{Z}^{(b)}.

ForπoutX\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}andπoutZ\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}, we defer to the link qubits to define the logical class, since the reduced Pauli group restricted to just the link qubit states is in 1-to-1 correspondence with the4N4^{N}“outer” logical classes.
Specifically, for any bit stringxout∈𝔽2noutx_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}, we defineπoutX​(xout)∈𝔽2kout\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}(x_{\mathrm{out}})\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}}to be the bit string consisting of just the link bits ofxoutx_{\mathrm{out}}.
For theZZcoordinates, letGout∈𝔽2kout×noutG_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}\times n_{\mathrm{out}}}be a full-rank generator matrix forCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}, and defineπoutZ​(zout)≡Gout​zout.\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}(z_{\mathrm{out}})\equiv G_{\mathrm{out}}z_{\mathrm{out}}.(B.67)

Based on these constructions, it’s not difficult to see that for any Pauli string withXXandZZcoordinates(x,z)∈𝔽22​n(x,z)\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{2n}, the logical coordinatesxL\displaystyle x_{\mathrm{L}}≡πoutX​(πinX​(xin(1)),…,πinX​(xin(nout))),\displaystyle\equiv\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{in}}\left(x^{(1)}_{\mathrm{in}}\right),\dots,\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{in}}\left(x^{(n_{\mathrm{out}})}_{\mathrm{in}}\right)\right),(B.68)zL\displaystyle z_{\mathrm{L}}≡πoutZ​(πinZ​(zin(1)),…,πinZ​(zin(nout))),\displaystyle\equiv\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{in}}\left(z^{(1)}_{\mathrm{in}}\right),\dots,\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{in}}\left(z^{(n_{\mathrm{out}})}_{\mathrm{in}}\right)\right),(B.69)

exactly track the space of all possible stabilizer cosets, when equipped with arbitrary syndrome representatives{Em}\{E_{m}\}.
We thus define and analyze our decoders in the physical coordinate system defined by these logical coordinates (in addition to the syndrome coordinates).

All error models we consider are i.i.d. single-qubit Pauli noise, which takes the formℰ=∏i=1nℰi\mathcal{E}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{i}, withℰi​(ρ)=(1−pX−pY−pZ)​ρ+pX​X​ρ​X+pY​Y​ρ​Y+pZ​Z​ρ​Z,\mathcal{E}_{i}(\rho)=(1-p_{X}-p_{Y}-p_{Z})\rho+p_{X}X\rho X+p_{Y}Y\rho Y+p_{Z}Z\rho Z,(B.70)

where0≤pX,pY,pZ≤10\leq p_{X},p_{Y},p_{Z}\leq 1are the physical rates ofXX,YY, andZZerrors, respectively, andpX+pY+pZ≤1p_{X}+p_{Y}+p_{Z}\leq 1.
IfpXp_{X},pYp_{Y}, andpZp_{Z}are collectively too large (e.g., with sum greater than1/21/2), then the most ideal decoder would always be forced to assume the unnatural hypothesis that some error has occurred, even in the absence of any nontrivial syndrome.
Therefore, in the following discussion, we will make the additional assumption that the physical error rates are constrained to the regime where errors at each step of decoding are less likely than no error.

## B.4.1Depolarizing Noise:ZZMemory Experiment

For the case of depolarizing noise, we takepX=pY=pZ=13​pp_{X}=p_{Y}=p_{Z}=\frac{1}{3}p, whereppis the overall physical error rate.
We assumeppis below the cutoff needed so that the default assumption on each qubit is that an error is less likely to occur than it is tonotoccur.
As we will see,p<1/2p<1/2is sufficient.

Importantly, in the case ofZZmemory, theZZcoordinates of the logical class have no effect on the recovery, because the original state is already an eigenstate of every logicalZZoperator.
Therefore, it suffices to provide the decoding strategy just for theXXcoordinates of the logical class.
FromSectionB.3.2, recall that we need to findxLx_{\mathrm{L}}that maximizesPr⁡[xL∣s]∝∑xout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xoutxL=πoutX​(xout)∏b=1noutPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=(xout)b∣sin(b)].\Pr\left[x_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]\propto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\\
x_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}(x_{\mathrm{out}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\Pr[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}].(B.71)

In the case of Gauss’s law codes, this simplifies substantially.
Note that the constraintxL=πoutX​(xout)x_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}(x_{\mathrm{out}})fixes the link bits ofxoutx_{\mathrm{out}}, and the constraintsout=Hout​xouts_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}subsequently fixes the site bits.
Therefore, only one term contributes in the sum, and the decoder objective simplifies toxL∗\displaystyle x_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=πoutX​(xout∗),\displaystyle=\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(x_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}\right),(B.72)xout∗\displaystyle x_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}≡arg​maxxout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xout​∏b=1noutPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=(xout)b∣sin(b)].\displaystyle\equiv\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\Pr[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}].(B.73)

This can be computed explicitly by the following observation:Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=0∣𝐬in(b)≠0]=Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1∣𝐬in(b)≠0]=12.\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\neq 0\right]=\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\neq 0\right]=\frac{1}{2}.(B.74)

That is, if the inner syndromesin(b)s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}on a given block is nontrivial, then theXXcoordinate(𝐱out)b≡πinX​(𝐱in(b))(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\equiv\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{in}}\left(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right)for the logical class on that inner block becomes a fair coin toss.
The reason for this is that a nontrivial inner block syndromesin(b)=Hin​zin(b)≠0s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=H_{\mathrm{in}}z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\neq 0implies that at least one of theZZcoordinates in the vectorzin(b)z_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}is nonzero.
If, say, thejthj^{\mathrm{th}}coordinate is nonzero, then the Pauli substring acting on thebthb^{\mathrm{th}}blockcannothave itsjthj^{\mathrm{th}}tensor factor as a pure bit-flip,XjX_{j}.
Instead, this factor must either beYjY_{j}orZjZ_{j}, both of which have exactly the same likelihood, since their difference is invisible to the inner block syndrome vectorsin(b)s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}.
But the two optionsYjY_{j}andZjZ_{j}also correspond to the two different inner logicalXXcoordinates, because they contribute different parities of bit-flip weights, thereby implying (B.74).

To compute the probabilities conditioned on the trivial inner block syndrome,Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=0∣𝐬in(b)=0],\displaystyle\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right],(B.75)Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1∣𝐬in(b)=0],\displaystyle\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right],(B.76)

we can use the relationPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1]=\displaystyle\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\right]=Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1∣𝐬in(b)=0]×Pr⁡[𝐬in(b)=0]\displaystyle\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right]\times\Pr\left[\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right](B.77)+Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1∣𝐬in(b)≠0]×Pr⁡[𝐬in(b)≠0].\displaystyle+\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\neq 0\right]\times\Pr\left[\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\neq 0\right].(B.78)

The LHS is computing the probability that the random bit string𝐱in(b)\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}has odd Hamming weight.
Under depolarizing noise, eachXXcoordinate is independently nonzero with probabilitypX+pY=23​pp_{X}+p_{Y}=\frac{2}{3}p, because bothXXandYYerrors have nonzeroXXcoordinate.
Therefore, this is precisely the probability that add-bit error generated by the BSC with physical bit-flip ratepbf≡23​pp_{\mathrm{bf}}\equiv\frac{2}{3}phas odd weight.
This is a well-known elementary combinatorial problem with answerPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1]=12​[1−f​(pbf)d],\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left[1-f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}\right],(B.79)

wheref​(x)≡1−2​xf(x)\equiv 1-2x.
Meanwhile, the inner syndrome is trivial if and only if all of theZZcoordinates on the block are equal.
Since eachZZcoordinate of the error is independently nonzero with probabilityppf≡pY+pZ=23​pp_{\mathrm{pf}}\equiv p_{Y}+p_{Z}=\frac{2}{3}p, we havePr⁡[𝐬in(b)=0]=g​(ppf),\Pr\left[\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right]=g(p_{\mathrm{pf}}),(B.80)

whereg​(x)≡(1−x)d+xdg(x)\equiv(1-x)^{d}+x^{d}.
Using (B.74) in the preceding relation and solving for the remaining conditional probability givesPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1∣𝐬in(b)=0]\displaystyle\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right]=Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1]−12​Pr⁡[𝐬in(b)≠0]Pr⁡[𝐬in(b)=0]\displaystyle=\frac{\Pr[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1]-\frac{1}{2}\Pr[\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\neq 0]}{\Pr[\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0]}(B.81)=12​[1−f​(pbf)dg​(ppf)].\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[1-\frac{f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}}{g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})}\right].(B.82)

Similarly,Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=0∣𝐬in(b)=0]=12​[1+f​(pbf)dg​(ppf)].\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left[1+\frac{f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}}{g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})}\right].(B.83)

Thus, conditioned on a trivial inner syndrome, the inner logicalXXcoordinate0is more likely than the inner logicalXXcoordinate11.
Conditioned on a nontrivial inner syndrome, the two coordinates are equally likely.

Now definew0​(sin)w_{0}(s_{\mathrm{in}})to be the number of blocksbbsuch thatsin(b)=0s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0, and definew0​(xout,sin)w_{0}(x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})to be the number of blocksbbsuch thatsin(b)=0s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=0and(xout)b=1(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1.
Then it follows thatxout∗\displaystyle x_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}=arg​maxxout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xout⁡12nout​[1−f​(pbf)dg​(ppf)]w0​(xout,sin)​[1+f​(pbf)dg​(ppf)]w0​(sin)−w0​(xout,sin)\displaystyle=\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\left[1-\frac{f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}}{g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})}\right]^{w_{0}(x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})}\left[1+\frac{f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}}{g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})}\right]^{w_{0}(s_{\mathrm{in}})-w_{0}(x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})}(B.84)=arg​maxxout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xout(1−f​(pbf)d/g​(ppf)1+f​(pbf)d/g​(ppf))w0​(xout,sin)\displaystyle=\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1-f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}/g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})}{1+f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}/g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})}\right)^{w_{0}(x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})}(B.85)=arg​minxout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xout⁡w0​(xout,sin),\displaystyle=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}w_{0}(x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}}),(B.86)

where we made the assumption thatf​(pbf)d/g​(ppf)>0f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}/g(p_{\mathrm{pf}})>0, which holds as long asp<1/2p<1/2.
In other words, an algorithm for the ML decoder with the concatenation structure is obtained by searching for thexout∈𝔽2noutx_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}that minimizes the number of bits turned onat the block indices where a trivial inner syndrome is observed, conditioned on having the correct outer syndrome.
This can be thought of as a modification to the classical MW decoder, which would have simply searched forxout∈𝔽2noutx_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}to minimize the number of bits turned onat all, conditioned on having the correct syndrome.

## B.4.2Depolarizing Noise:XXMemory Experiment

In the case ofXXmemory, it is nowxLx_{\mathrm{L}}which does not need to be considered.
Apart from this change, we proceed by similar logic to the previous case, and continue using the same notation.
We need to findzLz_{\mathrm{L}}that maximizes the marginal likelihoodPr⁡[zL∣s]∝∑xout,zout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xoutzL=πoutZ​(zout)∏b=1noutPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=(xout)b∧(𝐳out)b=(zout)b∣sin(b)].\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]\propto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}},z_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\\
z_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}(z_{\mathrm{out}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\Pr[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\wedge(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}].(B.87)

In fact, as we will show, it turns out thatzL∗≡arg​maxzL∈𝔽2k⁡Pr⁡[zL∣s]=0,z_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}\equiv\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{z_{\mathrm{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k}}\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]=0,(B.88)

holds exactly forp<1/2p<1/2.
In light of (B.64), this implies that the ML decoding with the concatenated structure can be accomplished by simply using an MW decoder for the classicaldd-bit repetition code to correct phase-flip errors independently on each inner block.

The first observation to this end is that if we defineqb​(t′;t)≡Pr⁡[(𝐳out)b=t′∣(𝐱out)b=t∧𝐬in(b)=sin(b)],q_{b}\left(t^{\prime};t\right)\equiv\Pr\left[(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=t^{\prime}\mid(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=t\wedge\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}=s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right],(B.89)

thenqb​(0;t)>qb​(1;t),q_{b}\left(0;t\right)>q_{b}\left(1;t\right),(B.90)

for all choices oft∈𝔽2t\in\mathbb{F}_{2}andsin(b)∈𝔽2nin−kins_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{in}}-k_{\mathrm{in}}}.
Intuitively, this makes sense:(𝐳out)b=0(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0corresponds to a Pauli error with low weight in theZZcoordinates, which is more likely to occur than an error with high weight in theZZcoordinates.
The complication is that this holds true even after we condition on knowledge of the inner syndrome𝐬in(b)\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}andthe inner logicalXXcoordinate(𝐱out)b(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}, irrespective of their values.
Despite the complication, this can easily be verified (by simply checking all possible cases).

Now writeR​(xout)\displaystyle R(x_{\mathrm{out}})≡∏b=1noutrb​((xout)b),\displaystyle\equiv\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}r_{b}((x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}),(B.91)rb​(t)\displaystyle r_{b}(t)≡Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=t∣sin(b)],\displaystyle\equiv\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=t\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right],(B.92)

so thatPr⁡[zL∣s]=∑xout,zout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xoutzL=πoutZ​(zout)R​(xout)​∏b=1noutqb​(1;(xout)b)(zout)b​qb​(0;(xout)b)1−(zout)b.\displaystyle\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}},z_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\\
z_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}(z_{\mathrm{out}})\end{subarray}}R(x_{\mathrm{out}})\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}q_{b}(1;(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})^{(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}}q_{b}(0;(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})^{1-(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}}.(B.93)

Note that∑zout∈𝔽2noutzL=πoutZ​(zout)∏b=1noutqb​(1;(xout)b)(zout)b​qb​(0;(xout)b)1−(zout)b=Pr⁡[𝐳L=zL∣𝐱out=xout∧𝐬in=sin],\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
z_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}(z_{\mathrm{out}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}q_{b}(1;(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})^{(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}}q_{b}(0;(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})^{1-(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}}=\Pr\left[\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}=z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}=x_{\mathrm{out}}\wedge\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}=s_{\mathrm{in}}\right],(B.94)

which we simply denote asp​(xL∣xout,sin)p(x_{\mathrm{L}}\mid x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})for brevity.
Therefore,Pr⁡[zL∣s]∝∑xout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xoutR​(xout)​p​(zL∣xout,sin).\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]\propto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}R(x_{\mathrm{out}})p(z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}}).(B.95)

We will show thatp​(0∣xout,sin)≥p​(zL∣xout,sin)p(0\mid x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})\geq p(z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}})(B.96)

for every choice ofxoutx_{\mathrm{out}}andsins_{\mathrm{in}}.
Since everyR​(xout)R(x_{\mathrm{out}})is clearly non-negative, this implies thatPr⁡[0∣s]≥Pr⁡[zL∣s]\Pr[0\mid s]\geq\Pr[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s]for everyzLz_{\mathrm{L}}, establishing the desired conclusion thatzL∗=0z_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=0suffices for the ML decoding with the concatenated structure.

To proceed further, let us defineCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}to be the dual code ofCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}.
ThenCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}is also the row span ofHoutH_{\mathrm{out}}.
More importantly, since𝐳L=πoutZ​(𝐳out)=Gout​𝐳out\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi_{\mathrm{out}}^{Z}(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})=G_{\mathrm{out}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}}, it follows that inequivalent samples of𝐳L\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}exactly correspond to samples of𝐳out\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}}that are drawn from differentCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}-cosets.
At an intuitive level, all this is saying is that twoZZ-type Pauli strings are in the same logical equivalence class if their quotient is in the group of outer stabilizer generators.
Mathematically, the statement is that for anyzL∈𝔽2koutz_{\mathrm{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}}, one can assignvzL∈𝔽2noutv_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}such thatπoutZ​(zout)=zL⇔zout∈vzL+Cout⟂,\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(z_{\mathrm{out}}\right)=z_{\mathrm{L}}\iff z_{\mathrm{out}}\in v_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp},(B.97)

wherevzL+Cout⟂v_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}denotes the coset ofCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}given by translating withvzLv_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}.
Since everyzLz_{\mathrm{L}}now uniquely corresponds to some coset defined byvzLv_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}, we are thus reduced to an optimization problem over these cosets.

To simplify the notation, fixxout∈𝔽2noutx_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}and let𝐙^\hat{\mathbf{Z}}denote the random variable taking values in𝔽2nout\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}with bits𝐙^b\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}drawn i.i.d. from the distributionPr⁡[𝐙^b=Z^b]=qb​(Z^b;(xout)b),\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}=\hat{Z}_{b}\right]=q_{b}(\hat{Z}_{b};(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}),(B.98)

for1≤b≤nout1\leq b\leq n_{\mathrm{out}}.
This immediately impliesp​(zL∣xout,sin)=Pr⁡[𝐙^∈vzL+Cout⟂].p\left(z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid x_{\mathrm{out}},s_{\mathrm{in}}\right)=\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\in v_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}\right].(B.99)

As mentioned, the question reduces to the relative likelihoods for the cosets in which𝐙^\hat{\mathbf{Z}}can live.
We will show that the highest probability mass resides in the zero coset, i.e.,Cout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}itself.

For this, letv∈𝔽2noutv\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}be arbitrary, and define the indicator function of the desired event,Indv​(Z^)≡{1,Z^∈v+Cout⟂,0,Z^∉v+Cout⟂.\mathrm{Ind}_{v}\left(\hat{Z}\right)\equiv\begin{cases}1,&\hat{Z}\in v+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp},\\
0,&\hat{Z}\notin v+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}.\end{cases}(B.100)

We then employ the commonly used expansion of this indicator function,Indv​(Z^)=1|Cout|​∑u∈Cout(−1)u⋅(v+Z^),\mathrm{Ind}_{v}\left(\hat{Z}\right)=\frac{1}{|C_{\mathrm{out}}|}\sum_{u\in C_{\mathrm{out}}}(-1)^{u\cdot\left(v+\hat{Z}\right)},(B.101)

where we technically rely on the fact that the dual of the dual ofCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}is againCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}.
Then it followsPr⁡[𝐙^∈v+Cout⟂]\displaystyle\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\in v+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}\right]=𝔼​[Indv​(𝐙^)]\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Ind}_{v}\left(\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\right)\right](B.102)=1|Cout|​∑u∈Cout(−1)u⋅v​𝔼​[(−1)u⋅𝐙^]\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|C_{\mathrm{out}}|}\sum_{u\in C_{\mathrm{out}}}(-1)^{u\cdot v}\mathbb{E}\left[(-1)^{u\cdot\hat{\mathbf{Z}}}\right](B.103)=1|Cout|​∑u∈Cout(−1)u⋅v​∏b=1nout𝔼​[(−1)ub​𝐙^b],\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|C_{\mathrm{out}}|}\sum_{u\in C_{\mathrm{out}}}(-1)^{u\cdot v}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathbb{E}\left[(-1)^{u_{b}\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}}\right],(B.104)

where we used independence of the bits of𝐙^\hat{\mathbf{Z}}.
Conditioned on the value of the bitubu_{b}, it’s easy to see that𝔼​[(−1)ub​𝐙^b]={1,ub=0,qb​(0;(xout)b)−qb​(1;(xout)b),ub=1.\mathbb{E}\left[(-1)^{u_{b}\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}}\right]=\begin{cases}1,&u_{b}=0,\\
q_{b}(0;(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})-q_{b}(1;(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}),&u_{b}=1.\end{cases}(B.105)

Now we come to the critical point of the argument.
Recall that there was a slight bias towards lower weightZZcoordinates on physical Pauli errors, even when conditioned on arbitrary observation of the syndrome andXXcoordinates.
This culminated in the mathematical result (B.90), which holds irrespective of the values of(xout)b(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}and the measured inner syndrome.
But this implies that the above expectation value𝔼​[(−1)ub​𝐙^b]\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{u_{b}\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}}]ispositive(in fact, we only need it to be non-negative for the following argument to carry through).
Therefore,1|Cout|​∑u∈Cout(−1)u⋅v​∏b=1nout𝔼​[(−1)ub​𝐙^b]≤1|Cout|​∑u∈Cout∏b=1nout𝔼​[(−1)ub​𝐙^b],\frac{1}{|C_{\mathrm{out}}|}\sum_{u\in C_{\mathrm{out}}}(-1)^{u\cdot v}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathbb{E}\left[(-1)^{u_{b}\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{|C_{\mathrm{out}}|}\sum_{u\in C_{\mathrm{out}}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\mathbb{E}\left[(-1)^{u_{b}\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}}\right],(B.106)

since the RHS just directly adds up all the positive contributions where the LHS might attach a minus sign.
But the RHS is precisely thev=0v=0case, i.e., probability that𝐙^\hat{\mathbf{Z}}lives in the zero coset ofCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}.
This establishes the desired result.

## B.4.3Uncorrelated X/Z Noise: Arbitrary Memory

In the case of uncorrelated X/Z noise, there exist parameterspX′p^{\prime}_{X}andpZ′p^{\prime}_{Z}with0≤pX′,pZ′≤10\leq p^{\prime}_{X},p^{\prime}_{Z}\leq 1such that131313In the main text, we assumepX′=pZ′=pp^{\prime}_{X}=p^{\prime}_{Z}=p. Here, we consider the more general case.pX\displaystyle p_{X}=pX′​(1−pZ′),\displaystyle=p_{X}^{\prime}(1-p_{Z}^{\prime}),(B.107)pY\displaystyle p_{Y}=pX′​pZ′,\displaystyle=p_{X}^{\prime}p_{Z}^{\prime},(B.108)pZ\displaystyle p_{Z}=pZ′​(1−pX′).\displaystyle=p_{Z}^{\prime}(1-p_{X}^{\prime}).(B.109)

This implies that the single-qubit channelℰi\mathcal{E}_{i}can be factored asℰi=ℰiX​ℰiZ\mathcal{E}_{i}=\mathcal{E}^{X}_{i}\mathcal{E}^{Z}_{i}, whereℰiX​(ρ)\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{X}_{i}(\rho)=(1−pX′)​ρ+pX′​X​ρ​X,\displaystyle=(1-p_{X}^{\prime})\rho+p_{X}^{\prime}X\rho X,(B.110)ℰiZ​(ρ)\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{Z}_{i}(\rho)=(1−pZ′)​ρ+pZ′​Z​ρ​Z,\displaystyle=(1-p_{Z}^{\prime})\rho+p_{Z}^{\prime}Z\rho Z,(B.111)

and therefore thatXXandZZerrors are appliedindependently(i.e., in an uncorrelated fashion) for each qubit.

This uncorrelated nature makes ML decoding relatively straightforward.
The point is simply that the joint law factorizes,Pr⁡[xL,zL∣s]=Pr⁡[xL∣s]​Pr⁡[zL∣s],\Pr\left[x_{\mathrm{L}},z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]=\Pr\left[x_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right],(B.112)

and each factor is separately equivalent to the corresponding marginal distribution in its own right.
In fact, the absence of correlations implies further thatPr⁡[xL∣s]\displaystyle\Pr\left[x_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]=Pr⁡[xL∣𝐬out=sout],\displaystyle=\Pr\left[x_{\mathrm{L}}\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{out}}=s_{\mathrm{out}}\right],(B.113)Pr⁡[zL∣s]\displaystyle\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]=Pr⁡[zL∣𝐬in=sin].\displaystyle=\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid\mathbf{s}_{\mathrm{in}}=s_{\mathrm{in}}\right].(B.114)

ForxLx_{\mathrm{L}}, we havePr⁡[(𝐱out)b=(xout)b∣sin(b)]\displaystyle\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}\right]=Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=(xout)b]\displaystyle=\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\right](B.115)=12×{1+f​(pbf)d,(xout)b=0,1−f​(pbf)d,(xout)b=1,\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\times\begin{cases}1+f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d},&(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0,\\
1-f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d},&(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1,\end{cases}(B.116)

wherepbf≡pX+pY=pX′p_{\mathrm{bf}}\equiv p_{X}+p_{Y}=p_{X}^{\prime}is the physical bit-flip error rate, andf​(x)=1−2​xf(x)=1-2x.
This follows from the elementary combinatorial result for the distribution of the parity of the weight of a randomdd-bit error string generated from the BSC with physical error ratepbfp_{\mathrm{bf}}.
Now it follows, using similar logic toSectionB.4.1, thatxL∗\displaystyle x_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=arg​maxxL∈𝔽2kout​∑xout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xoutxL=πoutX​(xout)∏b=1noutPr⁡[(𝐱out)b=(xout)b∣sin(b)]\displaystyle=\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{x_{\mathrm{L}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\mathrm{out}}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\\
x_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}(x_{\mathrm{out}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\Pr[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(x_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}](B.117)=πoutX​(xout∗),\displaystyle=\pi^{X}_{\mathrm{out}}\left(x_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}\right),(B.118)

wherexout∗\displaystyle x_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}≡arg​maxxout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xout⁡12nout​[1−f​(pbf)d]wt(xout)​[1+f​(pbf)d]nout−wt(xout)\displaystyle\equiv\operatorname*{arg\,max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}}\left[1-f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}\right]^{\operatorname*{wt}(x_{\mathrm{out}})}\left[1+f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}\right]^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-\operatorname*{wt}(x_{\mathrm{out}})}(B.119)=arg​minxout∈𝔽2noutsout=Hout​xout⁡wt(xout).\displaystyle=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
s_{\mathrm{out}}=H_{\mathrm{out}}x_{\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\operatorname*{wt}(x_{\mathrm{out}}).(B.120)

In other words, we have anexactMW decoder for the logicalXXcoordinates, provided that the bit-flip ratepbf<1/2p_{\mathrm{bf}}<1/2.

ForzLz_{\mathrm{L}}, the marginal simplifies asPr⁡[zL∣s]∝∑zout∈𝔽2noutzL=πoutZ​(zout)∏b=1noutPr⁡[(𝐳out)b=(zout)b∣sin(b)].\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]\propto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\\
z_{\mathrm{L}}=\pi^{Z}_{\mathrm{out}}(z_{\mathrm{out}})\end{subarray}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\Pr[(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}].(B.121)

Each factor counts the probability that a BSC with physical error rateppf≡pY+pZ=pZ′p_{\mathrm{pf}}\equiv p_{Y}+p_{Z}=p_{Z}^{\prime}generates add-bit error string with weight less thand/2d/2(if(zout)b=0(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0) or more thand/2d/2(if(zout)b=1(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1).
In analogy withSectionB.4.2, we defineqb​(t′)≡Pr⁡[(𝐳out)b=t′∣sin(b)],q_{b}(t^{\prime})\equiv\Pr[(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=t^{\prime}\mid s_{\mathrm{in}}^{(b)}],(B.122)

with the explicit formqb​(0)\displaystyle q_{b}(0)=∑w=0(d−1)/2(dw)​ppfw​(1−ppf)d−w,\displaystyle=\sum_{w=0}^{(d-1)/2}\binom{d}{w}p_{\mathrm{pf}}^{w}(1-p_{\mathrm{pf}})^{d-w},(B.123)qb​(1)\displaystyle q_{b}(1)=1−qb​(0),\displaystyle=1-q_{b}(0),(B.124)

which arises from summing the likelihood of every allowed weight of theZZcoordinate at blockbb.
It’s not too hard to see thatqb​(0)>qb​(1)q_{b}(0)>q_{b}(1), provided thatppf<1/2p_{\mathrm{pf}}<1/2, which puts us in the same position as inSectionB.4.2.
We will use this fact to show thatzL∗=0z_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=0, which implies that we have MW decoding for phase-flip errors, independently on every block.

Following the argument fromSectionB.4.2closely, we define the random variable𝐙^\hat{\mathbf{Z}}taking values in𝔽2nout\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}with i.i.d. bits drawn from the distributionPr⁡[𝐙^b=Z^b]=qb​(Z^b).\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}_{b}=\hat{Z}_{b}\right]=q_{b}(\hat{Z}_{b}).(B.125)

Then it again holds thatPr⁡[zL∣s]∝Pr⁡[𝐙^∈vzL+Cout⟂],\Pr\left[z_{\mathrm{L}}\mid s\right]\propto\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\in v_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}\right],(B.126)

where eachvzL∈𝔽2noutv_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}serves as a coset identifier corresponding tozLz_{\mathrm{L}}.
The exact same argument fromSectionB.4.2goes through, and we findPr⁡[𝐙^∈vzL+Cout⟂]≤Pr⁡[𝐙^∈Cout⟂],\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\in v_{z_{\mathrm{L}}}+C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}\right]\leq\Pr\left[\hat{\mathbf{Z}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}\right],(B.127)

establishing thatzL=0z_{\mathrm{L}}=0.

## Appendix CExact Calculation for Logical Error Rate

Now we come to the exact calculation of the logical error rate (LER) used inSectionIV.2.2, for an optimal11D Gauss’s law codeCout∘CinC_{\mathrm{out}}\circ C_{\mathrm{in}}whereCout=[nout,kout,d]C_{\mathrm{out}}=[n_{\mathrm{out}},k_{\mathrm{out}},d]is a classical Gauss’s law code for bit-flips andCin=[d,1,d]C_{\mathrm{in}}=[d,1,d]is a classical repetition code for phase-flips.
We focus on the case of uncorrelated X/Z noise, and use the strict version of the LER (IV.21).
Additionally, to reproduce the results inFig.13, it suffices to assumeddis odd.
This calculation is essentially a continuation fromSectionB.4.3, once we have selected the ML decoder based on marginal likelihoods.
Thus, we adopt the full notation conventions introduced inAppendixB.

The strict LER is given by the probability that the logical coordinates(𝐱L∗,𝐳L∗)(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*},\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*})determined from the ML decodingdo not exactly matchthe logical coordinates(𝐱L,𝐳L)(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}},\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}})drawn randomly from the distribution induced by the random Pauli error𝐄\mathbf{E}.
Note that we embolden𝐱L∗\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}and𝐳L∗\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}because they are now themselves random variables, since the output of the decoder is a function of the random Pauli error.
In the uncorrelated X/Z channel, theXXandZZcomponents of𝐄\mathbf{E}are independent, and therefore the events𝐱L∗=𝐱L\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}and𝐳L∗=𝐳L\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}are independent.
Therefore, the strict LER can be expressed asLER=1−pout⋅pin,\mathrm{LER}=1-p_{\mathrm{out}}\cdot p_{\mathrm{in}},(C.1)

wherepout\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{out}}≡Pr⁡[𝐱L∗=𝐱L],\displaystyle\equiv\Pr\left[\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}\right],(C.2)pin\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{in}}≡Pr⁡[𝐳L∗=𝐳L].\displaystyle\equiv\Pr\left[\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}\right].(C.3)

Let’s start with the case of inner decoding.
Recall that forppf<1/2p_{\mathrm{pf}}<1/2, the inner ML decoding step is exactly equivalent to MW decoding on the BSC with physical error rateppfp_{\mathrm{pf}}.
DefinePCin​(p)P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p)to be precisely the probability that an MW decoder succeeds for the classical codeCinC_{\mathrm{in}}(i.e., thedd-bit repetition code) on the BSC with physical error ratepp.
Then we have, for instance,PCin​(ppf)≡Pr⁡[(𝐳out)b=0],P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{pf}})\equiv\Pr\left[(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0\right],(C.4)

because the inner decoder always prefers(zout)b=0(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0on every inner block.
By iterating over all possible weights0≤w≤(d−1)/20\leq w\leq(d-1)/2on which the MW decoder would succeed, it’s not hard to see thatPCin​(p)=∑w=0(d−1)/2(dw)​pw​(1−p)d−w.P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p)=\sum_{w=0}^{(d-1)/2}\binom{d}{w}p^{w}(1-p)^{d-w}.(C.5)

Now, to get topinp_{\mathrm{in}}, note that the event𝐳L∗=𝐳L\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{L}}occurs if and only if the pattern of inner blocks on which the MW decoder succeeds perfectly corresponds to an element of the dual code,CoutC_{\mathrm{out}}.
In other words,pin\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{in}}=Pr⁡[𝐳out∈Cout⟂]\displaystyle=\Pr\left[\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}\right](C.6)=∑zout∈Cout⟂PCin​(ppf)nout−wt(zout)⋅[1−PCin​(ppf)]wt(zout),\displaystyle=\sum_{z_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}}P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{pf}})^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-\operatorname*{wt}(z_{\mathrm{out}})}\cdot\left[1-P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{pf}})\right]^{\operatorname*{wt}(z_{\mathrm{out}})},(C.7)

by summing the respective likelihoods over all the ways this can happen.

Let us assume we are dealing with anNN-site lattice, and writed=2​t+1d=2t+1for the code distance.
Then the sum (C.6) can be interpreted as the partition function for a11D system of binary variables with nearest-neighbor interactions, which can thus be evaluated exactly with a transfer matrix.

To understand this picture, we must first understand the structure of the dual codeCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}.
Recall that the classical Gauss’s law codeCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}used in the optimal construction requires duplicating site variablestttimes into the classical binary variablesSi(j)∈𝔽2S_{i}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}for1≤i≤N1\leq i\leq Nand1≤j≤t1\leq j\leq t; links are not duplicated, but we still denoteLi(1)∈𝔽2L_{i}^{(1)}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}for the classical binary variable corresponding to theithi^{\mathrm{th}}link, for1≤i≤N1\leq i\leq N.
Any proposed parity checkh∈𝔽2nouth\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}can be indexed by these duplicated (or not) site and link variables, wherehSi(j)=1h_{S_{i}^{(j)}}=1indicates thatSi(j)S_{i}^{(j)}participates in the parity check, andhLi(1)=1h_{L_{i}^{(1)}}=1indicates thatLi(1)L_{i}^{(1)}participates in the parity check.
The corresponding linear constraint for a givenh∈𝔽2nouth\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}would be∑i=1N(hLi(1)​Li(1)+∑j=1thSi(j)​Si(j))=0.\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(h_{L_{i}^{(1)}}L_{i}^{(1)}+\sum_{j=1}^{t}h_{S_{i}^{(j)}}S_{i}^{(j)}\right)=0.(C.8)

Not all such checks are elements of the dual codeCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}.
A convenient basis forCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}is provided byh(i)\displaystyle h^{(i)}:Li−1(1)+Si(1)+Li(1)=0,\displaystyle:L_{i-1}^{(1)}+S_{i}^{(1)}+L_{i}^{(1)}=0,(C.9)h(i,j)\displaystyle h^{(i,j)}:Si(1)+Si(j)=0,\displaystyle:S_{i}^{(1)}+S_{i}^{(j)}=0,(C.10)

where1≤i≤N1\leq i\leq Nand2≤j≤t2\leq j\leq t, and indices are identified periodically moduloNN, so thatL0(1)≡LN(1)L_{0}^{(1)}\equiv L_{N}^{(1)}.
Theh(i)h^{(i)}checks enforce Gauss’s law constraints, while theh(i,j)h^{(i,j)}checks enforce duplication of the site variables.
Writeαi\alpha_{i}andβi​j\beta_{ij}as the binary coefficients of anyh∈Cout⟂h\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}when expanded in this basis, so thath=∑a=1N(αi​h(i)+∑b=2tβi​j​h(i,j)).h=\sum_{a=1}^{N}\left(\alpha_{i}h^{(i)}+\sum_{b=2}^{t}\beta_{ij}h^{(i,j)}\right).(C.11)

The key observation from this basis expansion is that an arbitrary wordh∈𝔽2nouth\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}is an element of the dual codeCout⟂C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}if and only if there exist binary variablesα1,…,αN∈𝔽2\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{N}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}such thathLi(1)\displaystyle h_{L_{i}^{(1)}}=αi+αi+1,\displaystyle=\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1},(C.12)∑j=1thSi(j)\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{t}h_{S_{i}^{(j)}}=αi,\displaystyle=\alpha_{i},(C.13)

for every1≤i≤N1\leq i\leq N, where indices are again identified periodically moduloNN, so thatαN+1≡α1\alpha_{N+1}\equiv\alpha_{1}.

With these points noted, the transfer-matrix procedure for computing (C.6) can now be provided.
For anyzout∈Cout⟂z_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}, assign a weight contribution to each bit ofzoutz_{\mathrm{out}}as follows: if the bit is0, then assign weightW​(0)≡PCin​(pbf)W(0)\equiv P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{bf}}); if the bit is11, then assign weightW​(1)≡1−W​(0)W(1)\equiv 1-W(0).
In other words, define the functionW​((zout)b)≡{PCin​(pbf),(zout)b=0,1−PCin​(pbf),(zout)b=1.W((z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})\equiv\begin{cases}P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{bf}}),&(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=0,\\
1-P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{bf}}),&(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1.\end{cases}(C.14)

The full weight ofzoutz_{\mathrm{out}}is then the product of the weight contribution from each bit, and the inner success probabilitypinp_{\mathrm{in}}can be understood as a “partition function” given by summing these weights over everyzoutz_{\mathrm{out}},pin\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{in}}≡∑zout∈Cout⟂PCin​(pbf)nout−wt(zout)⋅[1−PCin​(pbf)]wt(zout)\displaystyle\equiv\sum_{z_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}}P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-\operatorname*{wt}(z_{\mathrm{out}})}\cdot\left[1-P_{C_{\mathrm{in}}}(p_{\mathrm{bf}})\right]^{\operatorname*{wt}(z_{\mathrm{out}})}(C.15)=∑zout∈Cout⟂∏b=1noutW​((zout)b)\displaystyle=\sum_{z_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}}\prod_{b=1}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}W((z_{\mathrm{out}})_{b})(C.16)=∑zout∈Cout⟂∏i=1N[W​((zout)Li(1))​∏j=1tW​((zout)Si(j))]\displaystyle=\sum_{z_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left[W\left((z_{\mathrm{out}})_{L_{i}^{(1)}}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{t}W\left((z_{\mathrm{out}})_{S_{i}^{(j)}}\right)\right](C.17)

In order to evaluate this, it benefits us to use the coordinatization ofzout∈Cout⟂z_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}^{\perp}in terms of the componentsαi\alpha_{i}andβi​j\beta_{ij}, as introduced previously.
This change of coordinates, valid on the dual code, is given explicitly by(zout)Li(1)\displaystyle(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{L_{i}^{(1)}}≡αi+αi+1,\displaystyle\equiv\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1},(C.18)(zout)Si(1)\displaystyle(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{S_{i}^{(1)}}≡αi+∑j=2tβi​j,\displaystyle\equiv\alpha_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\beta_{ij},(C.19)(zout)Si(j)\displaystyle(z_{\mathrm{out}})_{S_{i}^{(j)}}≡βi​j,\displaystyle\equiv\beta_{ij},(C.20)

where1≤i≤N1\leq i\leq Nand2≤j≤t2\leq j\leq t.
Then we can writepin\displaystyle p_{\mathrm{in}}=∑α∑β∏i=1N[W​(αi+αi+1)​W​(αi+∑j=2tβi​j)​∏j=2tW​(βi​j)]\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha}\sum_{\beta}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\left[W(\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1})W\left(\alpha_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\beta_{ij}\right)\prod_{j=2}^{t}W(\beta_{ij})\right](C.21)=∑α∏i=1N∑βi​2,…,βi​t[W​(αi+αi+1)​W​(αi+∑j=2tβi​j)​∏j=2tW​(βi​j)].\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{\beta_{i2},\dots,\beta_{it}}\left[W(\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1})W\left(\alpha_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\beta_{ij}\right)\prod_{j=2}^{t}W(\beta_{ij})\right].(C.22)

In the first line, the sum runs over all binary coefficient tensorsα∈𝔽2N\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{N}andβ∈𝔽2N×(t−1)\beta\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{N\times(t-1)}, where the second index onβ\betabegins at22.
The second line follows from the distributive property, whereβi​2,…,βi​t\beta_{i2},\dots,\beta_{it}are dummy variables that are independently summed over𝔽2\mathbb{F}_{2}.

Now fix an index1≤i≤N1\leq i\leq N, and consider just theβ\beta-dependent part of the sum in theithi^{\mathrm{th}}term of the product,Fi≡∑βi​2,…,βi​tW​(αi+∑j=2tβi​j)​∏j=2tW​(βi​j).F_{i}\equiv\sum_{\beta_{i2},\dots,\beta_{it}}W\left(\alpha_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\beta_{ij}\right)\prod_{j=2}^{t}W(\beta_{ij}).(C.23)

To evaluateFiF_{i}, defineγ1,…,γt∈𝔽2\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}byγa≡{αi+∑j=2tβi​j,a=1,βi​a,2≤a≤t.\gamma_{a}\equiv\begin{cases}\alpha_{i}+\sum_{j=2}^{t}\beta_{ij},&a=1,\\
\beta_{ia},&2\leq a\leq t.\end{cases}(C.24)

Then the choice ofβi​2,…,βi​t∈𝔽2\beta_{i2},\dots,\beta_{it}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}uniquely corresponds to a choice ofγ1,…,γt∈𝔽2\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{t}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}subject to the constraint∑j=1tγa=αi\sum_{j=1}^{t}\gamma_{a}=\alpha_{i}.
In other words,Fi≡∑γ1,…,γtγ1+⋯+γt=αi∏a=1tW​(γa).F_{i}\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{t}\\
\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{t}=\alpha_{i}\end{subarray}}\prod_{a=1}^{t}W(\gamma_{a}).(C.25)

From the definition of the weight function (C.14), it is clear thatFiF_{i}is computing precisely the probability thatttrandom bitsγ1,…,γt\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{t}drawn i.i.d. from the distributionBernoulli​(W​(1))\mathrm{Bernoulli}(W(1))have total parityγ1+⋯+γt=αi\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{t}=\alpha_{i}.
Therefore, we can invoke the well-known answer to this problem as used repeatedly inSectionB.4; namely,Fi≡12×{1+f​(W​(1))t,αi=0,1−f​(W​(1))t,αi=1,F_{i}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\times\begin{cases}1+f(W(1))^{t},&\alpha_{i}=0,\\
1-f(W(1))^{t},&\alpha_{i}=1,\end{cases}(C.26)

wheref​(x)=1−2​xf(x)=1-2x.
For brevity, let us denote this byF​(αi)≡FiF(\alpha_{i})\equiv F_{i}, since it depends entirely on the value of the bitαi\alpha_{i}.

Now the partition function simplifies topin=∑α∏i=1NW​(αi+αi+1)​F​(αi),p_{\mathrm{in}}=\sum_{\alpha}\prod_{i=1}^{N}W(\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{i+1})F(\alpha_{i}),(C.27)

which corresponds to a periodic, translation-invariant11D system with only nearest-neighbor interactions, where configurations are specified by the bit stringα\alpha.
This can immediately be computed by defining the transfer matrixMu​v≡W​(u+v)​F​(v),M_{uv}\equiv W(u+v)F(v),(C.28)

for indicesu,v∈𝔽2u,v\in\mathbb{F}_{2}.
The result ispin=tr⁡(MN)=λ+N+λ−N,p_{\mathrm{in}}=\tr(M^{N})=\lambda_{+}^{N}+\lambda_{-}^{N},(C.29)

whereλ±\lambda_{\pm}are the eigenvalues of the transfer matrixM=(W​(0)​F​(0)W​(1)​F​(1)W​(1)​F​(0)W​(0)​F​(1)).M=\begin{pmatrix}W(0)F(0)&W(1)F(1)\\
W(1)F(0)&W(0)F(1)\end{pmatrix}.(C.30)

Explicitly, these eigenvalues are given byλ±=12​[W​(0)±W​(1)2+(W​(0)−W​(1))2​t+1],\lambda_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}\left[W(0)\pm\sqrt{W(1)^{2}+(W(0)-W(1))^{2t+1}}\right],(C.31)

which now fully specifies the exact calculation ofpinp_{\mathrm{in}}.

Now we move onto the calculation ofpoutp_{\mathrm{out}}.
This requires understanding when exactly the event𝐱L∗=𝐱L\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}occurs.
Based on the construction inSectionB.4.3, it’s not difficult to see that𝐱L∗=πoutX​(𝐱out∗)\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\pi_{\mathrm{out}}^{X}(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}), where𝐱out∗\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}is the minimum-weight solution toHout​𝐱out∗=Hout​𝐱out,H_{\mathrm{out}}\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}=H_{\mathrm{out}}\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}},(C.32)

or equivalently, the minimum weight solution to𝐱out∗+𝐱out∈Cout\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}+\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}\in C_{\mathrm{out}}.
Note that the bit string𝐱out∗\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}is a random variable because𝐱out\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}is a random variable.

On the condition that (C.32) is satisfied, the event𝐱L∗=𝐱L\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}occurs if and only if𝐱out∗=𝐱out\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}.
This is because the equivalence𝐱L∗=𝐱L\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{L}}forces the link bits of𝐱out∗\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}and𝐱out\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}to agree, and (C.32) then ensures the syndromes agree, thereby forcing the site bits to agree as well.
In other words,pout=Pr⁡[𝐱out∗=𝐱out].p_{\mathrm{out}}=\Pr\left[\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}=\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}\right].(C.33)

Importantly, this isnotsimply calculating the probability that𝐱out\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}is the minimum-weight representative of its syndrome class.
This is because there can beseveralminimum-weight representatives, of which𝐱out∗\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}}^{*}will only be one of them.
An important concern that can be raised, then, is whether the success probability depends on the tie-breaking mechanism chosen by the MW decoder forCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}.
As we will see, there is no such dependence—the success probability is well-defined without choosing any specific MW decoder explicitly.

We can viewpoutp_{\mathrm{out}}as the success probabilityPCout​(p)P_{C_{\mathrm{out}}}(p)of the classical MW decoder on the classical Gauss’s law codeCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}, subject to a BSC with physical error rateppgiven byp\displaystyle p≡Pr⁡[(𝐱out)b=1]\displaystyle\equiv\Pr\left[(\mathbf{x}_{\mathrm{out}})_{b}=1\right](C.34)=12​[1−f​(pbf)d],\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[1-f(p_{\mathrm{bf}})^{d}\right],(C.35)

which is identical for every block index1≤b≤nout1\leq b\leq n_{\mathrm{out}}.
We have thus reduced the problem to a calculation in the setting of classical error correction.

For any syndromesout∈𝔽2nout−kouts_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-k_{\mathrm{out}}}, denoteA​(sout)A(s_{\mathrm{out}})to be the minimum weight of any error pattern having the syndromesouts_{\mathrm{out}}, and denoteB​(A)B(A)to be the number of distinct syndromessouts_{\mathrm{out}}such thatA​(sout)=AA(s_{\mathrm{out}})=A.
Without loss of generality, assume that for eachsouts_{\mathrm{out}}, the classical MW decoder deterministically returns the candidate error patterne​(sout)∈𝔽2noute(s_{\mathrm{out}})\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}.
Then the classical MW decoder succeeds if and only if the true error patterne∈𝔽2noute\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}happens to be drawn from the setS≡{e​(sout)∣sout∈𝔽2nout}.S\equiv\left\{e(s_{\mathrm{out}})\mid s_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}}\right\}.(C.36)

If𝐞\mathbf{e}denotes the random error pattern sampled from the BSC, thenPCout​(p)\displaystyle P_{C_{\mathrm{out}}}(p)=Pr⁡[𝐞∈S]\displaystyle=\Pr\left[\mathbf{e}\in S\right](C.37)=∑sout∈𝔽2nout−koutPr⁡[𝐞=e​(sout)]\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-k_{\mathrm{out}}}}\Pr\left[\mathbf{e}=e(s_{\mathrm{out}})\right](C.38)=∑sout∈𝔽2nout−koutpA​(sout)​(1−p)nout−A​(sout)\displaystyle=\sum_{s_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-k_{\mathrm{out}}}}p^{A(s_{\mathrm{out}})}(1-p)^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-A(s_{\mathrm{out}})}(C.39)=∑A=0AmaxB​(A)​pA​(1−p)nout−A,\displaystyle=\sum_{A=0}^{A_{\mathrm{max}}}B(A)p^{A}(1-p)^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-A},(C.40)

whereAmax≡maxsout∈𝔽2nout−kout⁡A​(sout).A_{\mathrm{max}}\equiv\max_{s_{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n_{\mathrm{out}}-k_{\mathrm{out}}}}A(s_{\mathrm{out}}).(C.41)

For general classical codes, evaluatingAmaxA_{\mathrm{max}}andB​(A)B(A)is believed to be computationally difficult[59,35].
Nevertheless, for the classical Gauss’s law codeCoutC_{\mathrm{out}}on anNN-site lattice with distanced=2​t+1d=2t+1, the exact values can be computed systematically.
